What this evaluates

(agb 0a8c4ee7) Which Claude model should run each planning step of agb (spec, architecture, task breakdown, test design)? Inputs are real agb projects at the moment the step began; every candidate runs agb's own stage command through the same wrapper; a check grades with agb's gates and a judge compares candidates pairwise.

This step (test-design): Test design: write a test plan (.ab/test-plan.json) that covers every acceptance criterion of the adopted task plan.

A pass of test-plan-gates means: The test plan covers every acceptance criterion of the adopted task plan.

directional

Recommend opus-5-5 · highest assurance

$3.2297 per success · required check 3/3 passed

2 eligible candidates tie at 1.000 (3/3) reliability and 1.000 (3/3) required-check pass rate (within the minimum meaningful difference 0.05); opus-5-5 is picked by the judge's pairwise score (opus-5-5 100%, next sonnet-5-5 0%).

Why, in the judge's words: On demo-todo, against sonnet-5-5: A provides stronger implementation guards through AT-020's PATCH bypass check, AT-028's concurrent-transition assertion, and AT-021's failed-reassignment rollback, beyond B's corresponding coverage. Its precise API outcomes and greater use of in-process UI tests outweigh the traceability deficit represented by its empty covers arrays.

The judge preferred opus-5-5 most (3 W 0 L 0 T of 3), which matches the recommendation.

Directional only: only 3 input(s) (fewer than 6). Also opus-5-5: required-check-pass-rate 3/3 has a 95% interval down to 0.439, below the gate 1; a gate of 1 can never be firm, declare one such as 0.95; opus-5-5: reliability 3/3 has a 95% interval down to 0.439, below the gate 1; a gate of 1 can never be firm, declare one such as 0.95.

Cost and success one point per candidate · up passes the required check more often, left costs less per success

The cost is the generation cost per success the operating mode ranks on: under "lowest cost" the recommendation is the leftmost filled point above the gate. A point can sit above the gate and still be hollow when another gate (reliability, pass^k) removed it; hover a point for its numbers and the gate that removed it.

Selection each eligibility gate removes candidates, the operating mode picks among the rest · rule select-v4

  1. required-check pass rate ≥ 100% — removed: nobody removed. Remaining: opus-5-5, sonnet-5-5.
  2. reliability ≥ 100% — removed: nobody removed. Remaining: opus-5-5, sonnet-5-5.

Passed every gate: opus-5-5, sonnet-5-5. The judge's win rate is for reference only and takes no part in selection.

Judge scores pairwise win rate · not used by the recommendation

No 1–5 scores: this step's methods does not declare absolute-1-5. That was declared, not missed.

Pairwise win rate (the table above) is a separate question: shown two outputs side by side, which did the judge prefer, 0–100, a tie counting 0.5. It only counts the matches each candidate took part in, so 0 means "lost every match", not "bad output".

Outputs side by side every candidate's answer to the same input, first trial, exactly the text the judge saw

Match evidence each dimension judged separately; an order disagreement is recorded as a tie by rule

opus-5-5 vs sonnet-5-5 demo-todo opus-5-5

A provides stronger implementation guards through AT-020's PATCH bypass check, AT-028's concurrent-transition assertion, and AT-021's failed-reassignment rollback, beyond B's corresponding coverage. Its precise API outcomes and greater use of in-process UI tests outweigh the traceability deficit represented by its empty covers arrays.

coverageopus-5-5 A tests bypassing the transition endpoint through PATCH, including atomic rejection of a combined title/status update (AT-020), and competing transitions with exactly one successful outcome (AT-028); B's AT-012 tests the transition table but neither of those enforcement gaps. A also tests reassignment rollback on failure (AT-021) and the distinction between an empty board and a board containing only completed tasks (AT-033), which B's reassignment and empty-board cases do not exercise.
falsifiableopus-5-5 A's AT-001 and AT-007 require specific HTTP statuses and error codes, whereas B's corresponding AT-020 and AT-025 accept generic 4xx rejection, allowing incorrect error classification to pass. A's AT-027 enumerates malformed transition bodies and requires 400 VALIDATION_ERROR with unchanged state; B's AT-037 bundles several endpoints and malformed inputs under a broader '4xx' and 'not corrupted' outcome.
levelsopus-5-5 A places detailed UI behavior such as transition-button rendering (AT-029), filtering (AT-040), and failed advancement (AT-043) in in-process integration tests, reserving real-backend E2E cases such as AT-044 and AT-046 for cross-page flows. B instead uses E2E tests for isolated rendering and mocked failures (AT-011, AT-019, AT-032, AT-033), and its AT-010/AT-011 repeat button-set assertions at unit and E2E levels.
traceablesonnet-5-5 Every B case has a nonempty covers array, while A leaves cases including AT-005, AT-022, AT-023, AT-034, and AT-043 without any criterion anchor. B's mapping is imperfect—AT-035 includes AC-06 for a deletion/reassignment race, whereas A's equivalent AT-009 uses AC-13 and AC-05—but B avoids A's numerous explicitly unanchored tests.
opus-5-5 vs sonnet-5-5 chessicle opus-5-5

A supplies stronger implementation guards through crash-safe upload idempotency in AT-031–032, consent ownership checks in AT-023, and server/UI timeout separation in AT-038–039. B has better anchor completeness, but permissive outcomes such as AT-061's zero-games handling and its narrower idempotency test AT-021 weaken its ability to catch real bugs. A wins despite its significant empty-covers traceability defect.

coverageopus-5-5 A AT-031–032 test upload idempotency against changed payloads, concurrent requests, and process crashes; B AT-021 tests only repeated submission with the same key. A AT-065 tests HTTPS and YouTube channel restrictions as well as domain allowlisting, whereas B AT-047 tests domain allowlisting alone. B does add a useful OCR accuracy threshold test in AT-027, but A covers more substantive failure and transition rules.
falsifiableopus-5-5 A AT-087 explicitly rejects gamesPlayed=0 and verifies that resubmitting the same period updates one row; B AT-061 permits zero to be either rejected or recorded 'as agreed.' A AT-055 and AT-072 require specific 404 responses and unchanged state for cross-user access, while B AT-022, AT-037, and AT-058 allow either 404 or 403. These concrete distinctions make A more likely to reject an incorrect implementation rather than accommodate its behavior.
levelsopus-5-5 A separates server timeout enforcement in integration AT-038 from slow-network presentation in unit AT-039; B combines both in unit AT-030, which cannot establish that the server actually stops the job. A likewise separates browser support classification and routing into unit AT-010–011, while B uses a mocked e2e test in AT-009. A reserves real-backend e2e cases such as AT-047 for navigation across upload, confirmation, and report pages.
traceablesonnet-5-5 Every B case has a nonempty covers array, including browser support AT-009, pagination AT-035–036, and partial-report recovery AT-043 and AT-052. Their counterparts in A include empty covers arrays in AT-010–011, AT-052–054, and AT-068–070 and AT-074, leaving those tests without criterion anchors despite descriptive titles. Neither output supplies explicit implementation task IDs, so B's consistently populated criterion anchors give it the advantage.
opus-5-5 vs sonnet-5-5 invoice opus-5-5

A provides stronger implementation guards through concrete replay and rollback tests in AT-035–042 and real-PostgreSQL concurrency checks for overlapping settlements, editing versus sending, and stopping versus scheduling in AT-066, AT-098, and AT-111. B is better anchored, but weaker assertions such as AT-012's successful edit without checking changed content and AT-053's unspecified '规则不允许的结果' make its smaller suite less dependable.

coverageopus-5-5 For send idempotency, A AT-038–042 separately test in-flight rejection, completed-result replay, changed-body rejection, and retry after failure; B AT-010 tests only concurrent duplication and changed-body rejection. For recurring dates, A AT-104 specifies successive month-end dates, leap-year recovery, and all five frequencies, whereas B AT-055 specifies the first monthly boundary but leaves its yearly and cross-year outcomes unstated.
falsifiableopus-5-5 A AT-068 requires an edited Draft's stored memo and items to change and totalAmount to become "12.50"; B AT-012 could pass with unchanged content provided the response succeeds and update_time advances. A AT-035–036 specify preserved fields, exact state transitions, one cancellation call, and a cleared paymentLink, while B AT-076 uses the much less discriminating outcomes '可重试' and not being stuck outside Sending/Voiding.
levelsopus-5-5 A separates component-visible validation from server enforcement, such as AT-030 versus AT-031 for missing payment tokens, and reserves AT-099 for a real-backend cross-page invoice flow. B AT-007 covers that missing-token rule only through mocked-backend e2e, and similarly uses mocked e2e for isolated empty states in AT-004 and AT-027 where A uses unit-level AT-020 and AT-144.
traceablesonnet-5-5 Every B case has a nonempty covers array, whereas A leaves substantial behavior unanchored, including authentication AT-001–009, client freezing AT-024–026, and payment processing AT-164–169. B's anchors are sometimes broad—for example AT-060 attaches three-list pagination to AC-001—but its corresponding behaviors do not float completely free of criteria as those A cases do.

Fields this run did not observe GAPS.md

  • ttft_ms: non-streaming calls; no time to first token.
  • cache: the provider reported cached prompt tokens on some calls; recorded under tokens.cached, no cache cost breakdown.
  • deployment_ref.region / tier: who served the model is read from the command's trace (cli/…), from OpenRouter's upstream provider, or from the model id a direct API answered with; region and service tier are not exposed.
  • human_intervention: not observed; no trace format read here says whether the agent waited on a person.
  • tool_calls, turns: observed for every trial from the trace the command left; counts in agent per row, the turn list in traces/.
  • checkpoint: not observed; no trace format read here says whether the agent resumed from one.
  • transport retry: model-API calls retry transient failures (HTTP 408/409/425/429, 5xx, network) twice with exponential backoff and no jitter; per-trial retry counts are not in scores.jsonl, only retry cost.
  • evaluator errors: none in this run.
  • absolute 1–5 scoring: **not run** — the spec declares methods without absolute-1-5. Magnitude is therefore not observed at all in this run; the empty score columns are a declaration, not a failure.
  • effective N: 0 of 3 inputs separated any two candidates on task success; 3 every candidate passed, 0 gave everyone the same partial rate. Only the separating inputs carry evidence for a choice (items.json). The judge preferred one candidate over another on 3 of 3.
  • pairwise judging uses only the first successful output per (candidate, input); repeated trials feed absolute scores only. The selector reads the pairwise score only to rank judge-preference, and to break a tie on assurance in highest-assurance.
  • statistical uncertainty: the chosen candidate's gated rates carry Wilson 95% intervals, and a highest-assurance pick must beat the runner-up on an exact sign test over inputs (recommendation.json evidence). Inputs generated from one template are still treated as independent, which overstates certainty when they share a template.
  • **combined run**: built from agb-flow-v3-2026-11-13T10-23-03-124Z (later runs supersede earlier ones per trial); nothing was generated or judged for it. combined-from.json says which run each trial came from.
